## Backlog #4 - SIEM export (CEF / LEEF / NDJSON)

| File | Responsibility |
|---|---|
| src/RdpAudit.Core/Siem/ISiemEventSerializer.cs | Single entry contract for the pure SIEM renderers: one `Format` method that turns the neutral SiemEvent sequence into the complete export text; defines `SiemFormat` (Cef / Leef / JsonNdjson). |
| src/RdpAudit.Core/Siem/SiemEvent.cs | Neutral SIEM record shared by the CEF / LEEF 2.0 / NDJSON serializers (one model, three renders); carries type, signature id, severity, UTC time, source/user fields, attack context and device identity. |
| src/RdpAudit.Core/Siem/SiemEventMapper.cs | Pure mapping from IPC projections (`IpEventEntryDto` / `AlertEntryDto`) to neutral `SiemEvent`; severity tables (failed-auth event ids = 5, High alert = 8, ...), EventCatalog-backed names and the caller-supplied device version. |
| src/RdpAudit.Core/Siem/SiemEscape.cs | Per-format neutralisation / escaping rules: CEF header (backslash, pipe), CEF extension key/value (backslash, equals, pipe), LEEF values (backslash, caret, pipe); embedded CR / LF / TAB become spaces. |
| src/RdpAudit.Core/Siem/CefSerializer.cs | Pure CEF renderer: `CEF:0` header, epoch-millisecond `rt`, `deviceCustomDate1` UTC time ("MMM dd yyyy HH:mm:ss +00:00"), `cn` / `cs` record-type fields. |
| src/RdpAudit.Core/Siem/LeefSerializer.cs | Pure LEEF 2.0 renderer: `LEEF:2.0` header, TAB-separated attributes, ISO-8601 `devTime` (`yyyy-MM-ddTHH:mm:ss.fffZ`). |
| src/RdpAudit.Core/Siem/SiemJsonSerializer.cs | Pure NDJSON renderer: one camelCase JSON object per line, strict `JavaScriptEncoder.Default`, no trailing newline on the last record. |
| src/RdpAudit.Core/Siem/UtcNormalizingDateTimeConverter.cs | JSON converter that re-marks `DateTimeKind.Unspecified` values (SQLite round-trip) as UTC so NDJSON always renders an explicit Z-terminated instant. |
| src/RdpAudit.Core/Siem/SiemSerializerFactory.cs | Factory mapping `SiemFormat` to a serializer plus `GetFileExtension` / `GetSaveFileFilter` / `GetDefaultFileName` for the SaveFileDialog. |
| src/RdpAudit.Core/Ipc/Contracts/AlertEntryDto.cs | MessagePack DTO projection of one `Alert` row for `GetAlertsForIp` (keys 0..8; `Details` deliberately omitted). |
| src/RdpAudit.Core/Ipc/Contracts/AlertsForIpRequest.cs | MessagePack request for `GetAlertsForIp`: `Ip` + `Limit`. |
| src/RdpAudit.Core/Ipc/Contracts/AlertsForIpDto.cs | MessagePack response for `GetAlertsForIp`: bounded newest-first alert window + total counter. |
| src/RdpAudit.Configurator/Services/SiemExportRunner.cs | Reusable "Export SIEM Events + Alerts" runner: IPC fetch (GetStatus / GetEventsForIp 39 / GetAlertsForIp 70) → neutral SiemEvent composition → SaveFileDialog → UTF-8-no-BOM write; pure `BuildText` for deterministic tests. |
| tests/RdpAudit.Core.Tests/Siem/CefSerializerTests.cs | CEF renderer golden-shape tests. |
| tests/RdpAudit.Core.Tests/Siem/LeefSerializerTests.cs | LEEF renderer golden-shape tests. |
| tests/RdpAudit.Core.Tests/Siem/SiemEscapeTests.cs | Escaping / neutralisation unit tests. |
| tests/RdpAudit.Core.Tests/Siem/SiemEventMapperTests.cs | Mapper tests: severity, event names, alert mapping, device version. |
| tests/RdpAudit.Core.Tests/Siem/SiemJsonSerializerTests.cs | NDJSON renderer tests: camelCase, enum strings, UTC, no trailing newline. |
| tests/RdpAudit.Core.Tests/Siem/SiemSerializerFactoryTests.cs | Factory + dialog-metadata tests. |
| tests/RdpAudit.Configurator.Tests/SiemExportRunnerTests.cs | `BuildText` ordering / status-skip / no-BOM write tests (CEF, LEEF, NDJSON goldens). |

Every asynchronous page call runs through StaTestHarness. |
| tests/RdpAudit.Configurator.Tests/StaTestHarness.cs | STA test host for the WinForms tab-page tests. Pumps Application.DoEvents() in a bounded loop instead of blocking the thread that owns the page's WindowsFormsSynchronizationContext - blocking that thread deadlocks the page against its own ConfigureAwait(true) continuation (measured: RefreshOverviewAsync never completed while blocked, completed in 2.03 s while pumped). Bounded by a 30 s test-body deadline, a 20 s per-operation deadline and a 2 minute per-test-class budget, on a background STA thread, so a regression fails the test instead of hanging the run. |
| src/RdpAudit.Configurator/Services/ConfiguratorExceptionGuard.cs | Configurator-side counterpart of the service's CrashGuard, installed by Program.Main before the first window exists. Attaches Application.SetUnhandledExceptionMode(CatchException), Application.ThreadException, AppDomain.CurrentDomain.UnhandledException and TaskScheduler.UnobservedTaskException, so an exception that escapes an async event handler (async void - no caller left to catch it) is journaled and reported instead of terminating the process and discarding unsaved state. Recoverable faults are shown to the operator behind a one-dialog-per-30-seconds throttle; a terminating AppDomain fault is written synchronously and no dialog is attempted. The journal is %ProgramData%\RdpAudit\logs\configurator-unhandled.log through the shared DiagnosticLogRotation helper; without a paths provider the sink no-ops (same discipline as CrashGuard) and an internal test-only override keeps the test suite out of the real ProgramData tree. |
| src/RdpAudit.Configurator/Services/SafeAsyncHandler.cs | The single sanctioned way to attach an async page action to a WinForms event. A lambda assigned to an EventHandler is async void at runtime, so an exception escaping it has no caller left to catch it; the wrapper catches it, journals it under the declaring page's name (ConfiguratorExceptionGuard) and runs the page's own last-resort failure report on the surface the operator is looking at, which never touches a disposed control. Promoted from DatabasePage's private AsyncClick so pages cannot drift apart on this contract; docs/30-configurator.md makes it the rule for new handlers. |
| tests/RdpAudit.Configurator.Tests/ConfiguratorExceptionGuardTests.cs | The global safety net: a throwing handler does not escape and lands in the journal; a UI-thread fault routed through Application.OnThreadException is survived, journaled and shown; the terminating AppDomain case is journaled with no dialog; an unobserved task fault is marked observed; repeated faults produce one dialog; Install/Uninstall are idempotent; and Program.Main's startup order (initialize WinForms, pin DPI, install) does not throw. The wiring tests run on a bare STA thread because WinForms latches the unhandled-exception mode once a control exists on a thread - which the StaTestHarness's synchronization context does. |
| tests/RdpAudit.Configurator.Tests/ConfiguratorAsyncHandlerHygieneTests.cs | The async-handler contract and its debt counter: the wrapper reports a faulted action (both the synchronously faulted and the after-an-await case) and never rethrows, the migrated pages attach no raw `+= async` handler and no named `async void`, and the remaining unguarded `+= async` sites are pinned per file (64 sites in 18 files after the B2 migration) so the debt cannot grow silently. |
| tests/RdpAudit.Configurator.Tests/ConfiguratorSyncOverAsyncGuardTests.cs | Enforces B1/F3: no GetAwaiter().GetResult(), no .Wait(...) and no .Result read on a task anywhere in src/RdpAudit.Configurator (CA2007 is suppressed repo-wide, so this test is the gate). One test proves by reflection - base types, fields, properties, events, method signatures, generic arguments and the types a body constructs - that the guarded types really are reachable from the UI types, so the guard cannot pass by being blind; the scan itself is whole-project (a superset of the reachable set) and carries an explicit allow-list that must state a reason for every tolerated finding. |
| tests/RdpAudit.Configurator.Tests/TestPathsIsolation.cs | Module initializer that redirects ConfiguratorExceptionGuard.Default's last-resort journal into a temporary root for the whole test process, so no test run appends to the machine's real %ProgramData%\RdpAudit tree (the RdpAuditPaths contract). |
| tests/RdpAudit.Core.Tests/NotificationOptionsTests.cs | Locks the Notifications section contract: safe defaults, clamping, severity fallback. |
| tests/RdpAudit.Notifier.Tests/NotificationFilterTests.cs | Locks the filter contract: severity gate, per-rule visibility, id deduplication. |
| tests/RdpAudit.Notifier.Tests/NotificationPolicyTests.cs | Locks policy resolution from the IPC DTO: clamping, severity fallback, defaults. |
| tests/RdpAudit.Notifier.Tests/NotificationPollerTests.cs | Locks the polling coordinator contract with a fake alert source: settings fetch, degraded delivery, dedup and the aggregate notice. |

## Stage 2a - Database tab, server side (IPC ordinals 80-84)

Server half of the Database tab: capacity visibility, retention governance, dry-run preview and the
deletion ledger. No UI in this stage (the Configurator page is stage 3), no backup/restore (2b), no
shard governance (4).

| File | Responsibility |
|---|---|

| src/RdpAudit.Core/Ipc/Contracts/RetentionPolicyDto.cs | Read contract for the Retention editor: every RetentionPolicyOptions field as configured AND as clamped-effective, the supported bounds, the global StorageOptions windows and the per-event EventRetention overrides enriched from the event catalog. |
| src/RdpAudit.Core/Ipc/Contracts/RetentionPolicyMutationRequest.cs | Mutation payload for SaveRetentionPolicy: an optional policy section, an optional global-windows section and a per-event override list with an explicit replace-all mode; a null section means "leave what is on disk unchanged". |
| src/RdpAudit.Core/Ipc/Contracts/RetentionPreviewDto.cs | Dry-run contract for PreviewRetention: the trigger that would fire, whether a real pass would act on it, the rows it would delete, the per-code counts with honest skip reasons and the planned steps in execution order. The reclaimed-bytes estimate is deliberately null with a stated reason. |

Deliberately has NO full-VACUUM member, so the request validator can refuse every value outside the read-safe set. |
| src/RdpAudit.Core/Ipc/Contracts/DatabaseMaintenanceDto.cs | Request (one kind plus an optional vacuum page budget, clamped server-side) and structured report (one step per executed pragma with outcome, detail and duration, plus the monospace ResultText the Configurator shows verbatim) for RunDatabaseMaintenance (IPC 89). |
| src/RdpAudit.Core/Ipc/Contracts/RestoreDatabaseBackupToDirectoryRequest.cs | Request for RestoreDatabaseBackupToDirectory (IPC 90): an optional snapshot name and the absolute scratch directory to restore into. The live store and the backup root are never named, which is what keeps the command safe. |
| src/RdpAudit.Service/Services/InformationDensityRetentionEngine.cs | The single implementation of the retention decision: trigger enumeration, the bounded statistics snapshot, the anchor/tail lookups, the planner call, the delete predicate, the batched delete-plus-ledger pairing and the protected-code inventory for a dry run. Both the background pass and the preview call it, which is what makes the preview a prediction rather than an estimate. Carries the internal AfterBatchCommitted hook (invoked right after a batch commits, null in production) so an integration test can cancel a pass at a deterministic point instead of racing it. |
| src/RdpAudit.Service/Services/DatabaseOverviewService.cs | Builds DatabaseOverviewDto in bounded, independently failable sections (DbFiles, ShardTree, DbCounts, Growth, StorePages, Schema, Ledger, Integrity, StorageAcl, Fingerprint, MigrationMarker, Backups), each with its own timeout; a degraded section marks the snapshot partial and names itself instead of failing the command. The StorePages section reads PRAGMA auto_vacuum, journal_mode, page_size, page_count and freelist_count on one connection and turns them into the sentence that tells the operator whether online reclamation can work on this store at all (B27). |
| src/RdpAudit.Service/Services/RetentionPolicyService.cs | Reads and writes the retention settings: reports configured vs clamped-effective values, validates and clamps a whole mutation before writing anything, persists through SettingsManager inside an open database transaction so an unwritable document leaves no override behind, and writes one OperationLog row with the old value, the new value and the invoking Windows identity. |
| src/RdpAudit.Service/Services/RetentionPreviewService.cs | Produces the retention dry run through InformationDensityRetentionEngine and projects it into RetentionPreviewDto, including protected codes that the eligibility scan deliberately never examines. Read-only on every path. |
| src/RdpAudit.Service/Services/ShardInventoryService.cs | Builds the read-only shard inventory (IPC 88): streams the IpEventSummary shard metadata, walks the actions root once with a bound and without following reparse points, cross-references the two so a file no summary points at is reported as an orphan, reads orphan headers within a budget, and returns honest lower bounds instead of failing when the walk truncates. Never writes, moves or deletes a shard file. |
| src/RdpAudit.Service/Services/DatabaseMaintenanceService.cs | Runs one read-safe maintenance action (IPC 89) over the live connection with a per-step wall-clock budget: the three checks with bounded result lines, a WAL checkpoint with its busy/frame counts, a bounded incremental vacuum and PRAGMA optimize. The vacuum step measures the freelist depth before and after, because PRAGMA incremental_vacuum(N) returns no result rows whether it released nothing or thousands of pages, and when it released nothing it names the cause - an empty freelist, or a store whose auto_vacuum mode is not INCREMENTAL together with the offline route (B27). Refuses every kind outside the defined set - a full VACUUM does not exist in this surface - and reports each step outcome instead of throwing. |

| tests/RdpAudit.Core.Tests/DatabaseGovernanceDtoTests.cs | Locks the Stage 2a wire contract: JSON round trips with camelCase names and enums as names, and the nullable members that carry an honest "unknown" (reclaimed-bytes estimate, failure-marker timestamps). |

| src/RdpAudit.Core/Backup/DatabaseBackupLayout.cs | Pure path math for the database snapshot layout: Backups\<yyyyMMdd-HHmmss>\db\rdpaudit.db plus manifest.json, a bounded -NN collision suffix so two snapshots inside one UTC second never overwrite each other, the sibling .partial working directory, and name validation through PathSafety so a snapshot path can never escape the backup root. No I/O. |
| src/RdpAudit.Core/Backup/DatabaseBackupManifest.cs | The serializable manifest that makes a snapshot self-describing and verifiable (schema version, kind, creation instant, source path, size, SHA-256, applied migration and count, product version, shard scope, key-table row counts) plus its JSON codec with the atomic temp-file-plus-move write. A manifest of another schema version, or one missing the creation instant or the digest, is refused rather than partially interpreted. |
| src/RdpAudit.Core/Backup/SchemaCompatibility.cs | The pure downgrade-guard decision: given the migrations a database reports as applied and the migrations the running binaries define, return Compatible / DatabaseAheadOfBinaries / HistoryUnavailable / HistoryInvalid with an operator-actionable message. No database, no filesystem, no options - the one decision that must never be wrong. |
| src/RdpAudit.Core/Backup/DatabaseSnapshotVerifier.cs | Read-only verification of a snapshot before anything trusts it: manifest readability, SHA-256 recomputation, PRAGMA quick_check on the copy, and the schema comparison against the binaries, reported as distinct verdicts (damaged file vs unreadable manifest vs incompatible schema). Also carries the shared helpers for hashing, quick_check, the EF migration history and the key-table row counts. Opens every database read-only with pooling disabled. |
| src/RdpAudit.Core/Backup/PreMigrationBackupProvider.cs | The seam that lets the Core schema initializer require a snapshot without depending on the Service: IsEnabled, ExpectedDirectory and one CreateAsync call, plus the result record that names the directory the snapshot was expected in when it could not be taken. |
| src/RdpAudit.Core/Config/BackupOptions.cs | Database snapshot settings: the operator-surface switch, snapshot count, free-space floor, shard inclusion, the pre-migration switch (on by default) and the backup root, each with a clamped Resolve* helper. | src/RdpAudit.Core/Ipc/Contracts/DatabaseBackupCreationDto.cs | Result of a snapshot request: what was written where, its SHA-256, how the snapshot count changed, which older snapshots retention removed or deliberately left alone, and the reason when it was refused. |
| src/RdpAudit.Core/Ipc/Contracts/DatabaseBackupListDto.cs | Newest-first snapshot inventory with the manifest verdict per entry (including why an unreadable manifest is unreadable), the effective backup settings, and the count of foreign directories in the backup root that database retention never touches. |
| src/RdpAudit.Core/Ipc/Contracts/DatabaseBackupVerificationDto.cs | Verification evidence and verdict for one snapshot, distinguishing a damaged file, an unreadable manifest, a missing copy and a schema ahead of the running binaries instead of collapsing them into one invalid flag. |
| src/RdpAudit.Core/Ipc/Contracts/DatabaseBackupRestoreReportDto.cs | Result of restoring a snapshot into a scratch directory: the file produced, the bytes written, the optional shard tree, and the verification the restore relied on, so "the restore ran" and "the restore produced a usable database" stay distinguishable. |

| src/RdpAudit.Core/Config/SubnetAggregationOptions.cs | Shard-cardinality-guard thresholds (enable, tree file threshold, new-file rate limit, rate window, low-volume row threshold) with a clamped Resolve* helper each, so a hand-edited appsettings.json cannot widen a bound. Disabled by default. |
| src/RdpAudit.Core/Storage/Sharding/ShardCardinalityGuard.cs | The cardinality-guard decision in isolation: it tracks per-source rows and new files inside a rate window, rolls the window over on its own, caps the tracked table under a spray, and answers whether a new source keeps its own file or shares a /24 (IPv6 /64) aggregate bucket. Takes the thresholds per call, so a saved policy is live without recreating the guard; composes no path and touches no file. |

A fact the sink cannot know is null or the documented -1. |

FromOptions is the only producer, so a client cannot display a value the service would not enforce. |
| src/RdpAudit.Core/Ipc/Contracts/ShardingPolicyMutationRequest.cs | Write contract of SaveShardingPolicy: the complete desired shard policy (including the subnet-aggregation thresholds) as a replacement, so the server can validate the whole document before writing any of it. |
| src/RdpAudit.Core/Ipc/Contracts/CollectShardGarbageRequest.cs | Request (DryRun, true by default - an omitted payload can never delete) and report of CollectShardGarbage: every orphan candidate with its disposition, the exact deleted paths, byte and record totals, the deletion-budget and truncation flags, and the per-file reason a candidate was kept. |

| src/RdpAudit.Service/Services/ShardGarbageCollectorService.cs | Collects orphan shard files: a dry run deletes nothing, an apply pass re-validates every candidate immediately before deleting it (unreferenced in IpEventSummary with the inventory's own normalization, not open in the live writer pool, still past OrphanGraceDays, contained under the actions root, last-write time readable), bounds the run to MaxDeletionsPerRun, and audits each removal with its own OperationLog row. Deliberately does not write EventDeletionLedger, which accounts for deleted event rows and could not be filled truthfully here. |
| tests/RdpAudit.Core.Tests/ShardingOptionsTests.cs | The shard capacity ceilings: every new option defaults to the pre-Stage-4 behaviour, a hand-edited document cannot widen a bound, and the per-shard byte ceiling converts to a ring capacity by integer division with a floor of one record. |

| tests/RdpAudit.Service.Tests/Storage/ShardCapacityGovernanceTests.cs | The Stage 4 ceilings against a real filesystem and a real IpEventSummary: the byte ceiling sizes a new ring and keeps eviction exact, an existing shard is never re-shaped, the byte budget and the disk floor refuse new files while open shards keep being written, every refusal is visible in its metric and in the capacity state, the file budget keeps its own reason, and the guard routes low-volume sources into a flagged /24 bucket (promoting a proven source back out) while the disabled guard behaves exactly as before. |
| tests/RdpAudit.Service.Tests/ShardGarbageCollectorServiceTests.cs | Orphan collection against a real tree: a dry run deletes nothing, an apply pass removes only shards past the grace period, a referenced shard is never even a candidate, a shard a live writer holds open is kept, a missing tree is reported as nothing to examine, and a failed audit downgrades the report instead of claiming a clean pass. |
| tests/RdpAudit.Service.Tests/IpcDispatcherShardingPolicyTests.cs | The Stage 4 commands through the real dispatcher: the inventory reports the effective policy and the live capacity state (and an honest null without a sink), a policy save round-trips into appsettings.json with an audit row naming the actor and the old and new policy, a negative value or an unusable actions root is refused with the document byte-for-byte unchanged and no audit row, above-maximum values are clamped and reported clamped, and an omitted collection payload is a dry run. |
| tests/RdpAudit.Configurator.Tests/DatabasePageShardsTests.cs | The Shards section on the STA harness: the tab exposes the whole policy editor plus the explicit compaction statement and no button that pretends otherwise, both mutating actions start disabled and stay disabled while the service is unreachable, a save shows the field-by-field old-to-new diff and writes nothing when declined, a no-change save does not prompt, the banner takes the danger face only for a real refusal, and the collection prompt names every candidate with a stated remainder past its cap. |
| tests/RdpAudit.Configurator.Tests/DatabasePageIntegrityTests.cs | B25 on the STA harness through the existing IIpcClient seam: each of the five chain states renders with the right severity (ok neutral, interrupted a warning, missing/unreadable/chain-broken an error), an unrecognised state escalates, and a verdict that was NOT obtained renders UNKNOWN rather than `ok` - unreachable service, rejected command, a DTO the service marked Unavailable, a partial snapshot whose Fingerprint section timed out and a null state. Also asserts the anomaly marker and its detail reach the banner and the report, that the banner is a docked band outside the scrolling report, and that a fresh page already refuses to look healthy. |
| src/RdpAudit.Core/Integrity/LedgerChainHasher.cs | The canonical, deterministic SHA-256 digest of one EventDeletionLedger row plus its link to the entry before it: covers the row content and the previous hash and never the row id, formats timestamps without a Kind suffix (SQLite stores wall-clock text, so including the kind would fail every verified row after a restart), and states openly that an unkeyed chain detects modification, deletion and reordering but not an attacker who rewrites the whole chain. |
| src/RdpAudit.Core/Integrity/LedgerChainVerifier.cs | Verifies the deletion-ledger hash chain and names exactly where it broke: modification, a row removed from the middle and reordering each break it at a specific entry, pre-chain (legacy) rows are counted as unverifiable rather than as tampering, and truncation of the newest entries is documented as the one attack a chain cannot see (the fingerprint comparison covers it). Pure over an ordered row list, so the start-up check, the periodic pass, the overview and the tests share one implementation. |
| src/RdpAudit.Core/Integrity/StoreFingerprintChain.cs | The fingerprint CHAIN (B19): an ordered list of fingerprint records where each record carries its own SHA-256 digest and the digest of its predecessor, plus the sealed tip that records how long the chain should be and what its head digest is. Verify() decides one of FIVE states - Ok, Missing (the sealed tip accounts for entries that are not on disk, i.e. a deleted tail), Unreadable (a record or the tip exists but cannot be read/unsealed, which now includes a record carrying an explicit null in a field every writer fills in, and a tip of an unknown layout version), ChainBroken (a digest mismatch, a broken linkage, a sequence gap, or a chain that contradicts the tip in the rollback direction) and Interrupted (B24: the chain holds exactly one entry more than the tip accounts for, the tip seals the chain without it, and a durable write intent names that entry - the shape a crash between appending an entry and re-sealing the tip leaves) - so "nothing recorded yet", "something recorded is gone" and "we were cut short while writing" never render the same way. The tip's EntryCount, HeadDigest, HeadSequence and FormatVersion are verified; SealedUtc is advisory and a divergence in it alone cannot move a verdict. Canonicalize() is total (no dereference, whatever the file says) and DescribeNullField() names the field that made a record unreadable. No unforgeability is claimed: a local administrator can delete both stores, and the one soft state is still loud and no easier to reach than the Ok an attacker gets by re-sealing the tip. |
| src/RdpAudit.Core/Integrity/StoreFingerprintChainVerdict.cs | B25/B24: the ONE place a reported store-fingerprint chain state becomes an operator-facing verdict - the wire name (`ok`, `missing`, `unreadable`, `chain-broken`, `interrupted`, plus the `unverified` a host reports when it could only read the pre-B19 document), the short phrase per state, and the escalation band (`Unknown`, `Ok`, `Warning`, `Error`). BandOf() maps `ok` to the neutral band, `interrupted` to a warning (a cut-short capture is reported, not accused) and EVERY other reported value to an error; an absent or blank state is `Unknown`, never `Ok`, and a value this build does not recognise escalates rather than washing out as a pass. WasReported()/BandForReport() make the "was a verdict obtained at all" decision once, from the producer status plus whether the verdict section completed plus the state, so the Database tab and the Diagnostic tab cannot grade one state differently. DescribeNotReported() gives every surface the same sentence for a verdict that was never obtained. Deliberately separate from StoreIntegritySeverity, which grades a start-up comparison finding. |
| src/RdpAudit.Core/Integrity/StoreFingerprint.cs | The durable store fingerprint (capture time, clean-shutdown flag, file sizes and write time, highest ingestion sequence, per-table row counts, applied migrations, ledger chain state) and the severity vocabulary of the anomalies derived from it. |
| src/RdpAudit.Service/Services/StoreFingerprintAlarmSink.cs | The off-ProgramData mirror for the fingerprint chain (B19): IStoreFingerprintAlarmSink plus the Windows event-log implementation that writes the chain's verdict and the clean-shutdown tip under the source the INSTALLATION registers (never created at runtime - that would need HKLM writes at service start), and a Null implementation that makes "nothing is mirrored" an observable state instead of a silent null. Bounded on purpose: only a non-Ok verdict and the shutdown tip are published, so the Application log does not grow with every maintenance pass. |
| src/RdpAudit.Core/Integrity/StoreIntegrityComparer.cs | The pure comparison behind offline-modification detection, with the false positive each rule avoids stated on the rule: a row-count fall is an anomaly only against a clean-shutdown baseline (retention deletes rows while the service runs), a migration list must merely be an extension (upgrades append), a smaller file is information rather than an alarm, and a broken or shorter ledger is always strong. |
| src/RdpAudit.Core/Security/StorageAclPolicy.cs | The expected DACL of the evidence tree as SDDL (protected, SYSTEM and Administrators full control, no Users) plus the textual check that a real ACL satisfies it, written as a pure function so install.ps1, the service's start-up verification and the tests cannot disagree about what hardened means. An ACL that cannot be read is reported as unverifiable, never as compliant. |
| src/RdpAudit.Core/Config/StorageIntegrityOptions.cs | Cadence of the scheduled integrity checks and of the store fingerprint: the cheap daily quick_check is on by default, the expensive full check is opt-in and only starts inside the configured maintenance window (with midnight wrap), and every bound is clamped. |
| src/RdpAudit.Core/Config/StorageEncryptionOptions.cs | The encryption-at-rest seam: Mode (None / VolumeManaged / SqlCipher), the NAME of the secret that would hold the key, and the rotation interval. No data is encrypted in this release; no key material ever appears here, in logs or in the settings document. |
| src/RdpAudit.Core/Config/StorageEncryptionGuard.cs | Fail-closed start-up validation of the encryption-at-rest setting: selecting SqlCipher, which this build does not implement, throws with an actionable message instead of letting the service run with plain-text files under a setting that claims encryption; VolumeManaged is accepted with an explicit warning that the product cannot verify the volume's state; an undefined numeric value is refused too. |
| src/RdpAudit.Core/Ipc/Contracts/DatabaseCompactionDto.cs | Request and answer of CompactDatabase (93): the always-Refused status, the offline procedure as a list of steps for verbatim display, and the store's read-only page accounting (page size, page count, freelist depth, reclaimable bytes) plus its auto_vacuum mode - the mode is what says whether an offline pass is merely worth stopping the service for or the only way this store will ever release free pages - so the refusal is actionable rather than merely negative. |
| src/RdpAudit.Core/Ipc/Contracts/PurgeBySubjectDto.cs | Contract of PurgeBySubject (94): the subject kind (IP, CIDR, login, UTC window), the mandatory dry-run flag, the typed phrase, and a report that separates the per-table counts from the operator-facing list of what the purge deliberately never touches. |
| src/RdpAudit.Core/Models/DatabaseIntegrityCheckResult.cs | One durable record per executed integrity check: what ran, when, how long it took, who caused it, and an outcome that never reports an unavailable check as a pass. It is a record of an observation; nothing repairs the store from it. |
| src/RdpAudit.Core/Models/DatabaseIntegrityCheckKind.cs | Which check produced a stored result (QuickCheck, FullIntegrityCheck, ForeignKeyCheck, LedgerChainVerify) and how it turned out (Ok, Warning, Failed). The ordinals are persisted, so they are an append-only ABI. |
| src/RdpAudit.Core/Data/Configurations/DatabaseIntegrityCheckResultConfiguration.cs | Maps the integrity-check journal and adds the single index the overview and the full-check cadence need: (Kind, CompletedUtc). |
| src/RdpAudit.Core/Data/Migrations/20260915124943_Stage18LedgerChain.cs | Stage 18 - adds PreviousHash and EntryHash to EventDeletionLedger (both nullable: rows written before the chain keep null and are reported as unverifiable). Additive only; Down drops the two columns. |
| src/RdpAudit.Core/Data/Migrations/20260915124943_Stage18LedgerChain.Designer.cs | Source module (no Purpose header). |

| src/RdpAudit.Core/Data/Migrations/20261023111504_AddAlertSourceIpDerived.Designer.cs | Source module (no Purpose header). |

| src/RdpAudit.Core/Data/Migrations/20261023204643_Stage20RawEventRecordId.Designer.cs | Source module (no Purpose header). |
| src/RdpAudit.Service/Services/EventDeletionLedgerWriter.cs | The single writer of EventDeletionLedger rows: both deletion paths (information-density retention and the operator's subject purge) append through it, so they compute the same canonical hash and their chains verify against each other. It runs on the caller's connection and transaction, so the tombstone and the deletion it accounts for commit or roll back together. |
| src/RdpAudit.Service/Services/PurgeBySubjectService.cs | Deletes every evidence row about one subject (IP, CIDR, login or UTC window): the dry run is the default and predicts exactly what an apply pass deletes, applying needs the typed phrase and is refused outright under a legal hold, deletes run in bounded batches with a whole-run ceiling, every table that loses rows gains a hash-chained ledger entry inside the same transaction, and the schema, migrations, configuration, bookmarks, ledger and operator enforcement state are never touched and are reported as such. |
| src/RdpAudit.Service/Services/DatabaseCompactionService.cs | Answers CompactDatabase (93) with the documented refusal: a full VACUUM on the live store is exactly what must not happen, and the service that answers an IPC call is by definition running. Read-only: it reports the store's page accounting so the operator can judge whether an offline compaction (the Configurator's path) is worth stopping the service for. |
| src/RdpAudit.Service/Services/StoreFingerprintStore.cs | Durable, crash-tolerant persistence of the fingerprint CHAIN (integrity\fingerprints\fingerprint-NNNNNNNNNN.json), of its sealed tip, of the durable write INTENT (B24) and of the anomaly marker, all written through a temp file plus an atomic move. The tip is protected through ISecretProtector (DPAPI on Windows), which is what makes a deleted chain TAIL visible - a prefix of a valid chain is a valid chain, so the chain alone cannot notice it. Write() is bracketed by the intent (written before the entry, removed after the tip), so a capture cut short between the two steps is reported as Interrupted instead of as tampering, and it completes a previous interrupted seal before appending anything, so at most one entry is ever unsealed and a persistent failure cannot drift into a rollback accusation. Reading never accepts an entry carrying an explicit null in a field this layout always writes, reports it as one that could not be read, and never throws. A pre-B19 single document is read once and migrated into the chain. Deliberately stored outside the database: a fingerprint kept inside the file it describes could be rewritten with the tampering it exists to reveal. |
| src/RdpAudit.Service/Services/StoreFingerprintVerdictReader.cs | B25: the ONE read of the fingerprint-chain verdict and the anomaly marker, used by both surfaces that show it (the Database-tab overview and the Diagnostic snapshot) so the two tabs cannot disagree about a tamper verdict. Reads the chain through the store the host provided, falls back to the pre-B19 document when no store was provided (reporting `unverified` and saying the timestamps are unverified rather than implying a pass), and truncates the anomaly marker to a bounded detail. Decides nothing: it returns ChainState/EntryCount/Message/MirrorAvailable/timestamps/marker, and the caller applies its own failure policy (the overview degrades to partial with a warning, the diagnostics section fails and is recorded in the section timings) - swallowing an I/O failure here would flatten "the marker could not be read" and "there is no marker". |
| src/RdpAudit.Service/Services/StoreIntegrityService.cs | Captures the store fingerprint, compares it against the clean-shutdown baseline at start-up (reporting anomalies to the log, to an OperationLog row and to a marker file the Configurator reads), adds the storage-ACL assessment to the same report, and runs the scheduled checks - each bounded, each journaled, the full check only inside the maintenance window. Purely observational: nothing here repairs the store. |
| src/RdpAudit.Service/Services/StorageEncryptionStartupNote.cs | Carries the non-fatal outcome of the encryption-at-rest assertion from configuration load to the point where the logging pipeline exists, since the assertion itself must run before the host is built. |
| src/RdpAudit.Service/Security/StorageAclEnforcer.cs | The runtime half of the ACL requirement: reads the DACL of the evidence tree, assesses it against the pure policy, and can re-apply it. Every failure to read or write an ACL is reported as unverifiable with the reason, because an organisation that denies ACL changes by policy must not be able to stop the service - and because "we could not look" must never read as "it is fine". |
| src/RdpAudit.Service/Workers/StoreIntegrityWorker.cs | Captures the store fingerprint as the service stops. Registered FIRST in the hosted chain so its StopAsync runs LAST, after every writer has stopped - a fingerprint taken any earlier would record live writes and the next start would report them as offline tampering. StartAsync is deliberately a no-op; the start-up comparison lives where the database is already migrated. |
| src/RdpAudit.Configurator/Services/LiveDatabaseSwap.cs | The single shared implementation of the only safe way to replace the live SQLite file with the service stopped (rename the database aside, move the -wal/-shm sidecars, move the validated replacement in through a staging file, roll the original names back on any failure). Shared by the restore and the offline compaction so the two cannot drift apart; the caller owns validation. |
| src/RdpAudit.Configurator/Services/DatabaseCompactService.cs | The offline compaction: WAL checkpoint, VACUUM INTO a copy next to the store, normalise that copy's auto_vacuum mode to INCREMENTAL (VACUUM INTO carries the source mode over, so a legacy store would otherwise be compacted back into the mode where online reclamation is a no-op - it costs one further VACUUM over the compacted copy and refuses below twice the copy's size free), verify the copy (quick_check, migration history, per-table row counts, foreign keys), then an atomic swap through LiveDatabaseSwap, keeping the pre-compaction file as a timestamped .bak and a receipt naming the mode before and after. Any mismatch refuses the swap, because the purpose is to shrink the file and never to change what is in it; it opens no pooled connection, because a pooled handle locks the file it is about to rename. |
| src/RdpAudit.Configurator/Forms/PurgeBySubjectDialog.cs | The subject-purge dialog: collects the subject, runs the mandatory dry run, renders the per-table counts and the preserved list, and keeps the Purge button disabled until a preview has succeeded AND the exact phrase has been typed (re-read at click time). A guard rail rather than the guard - the server re-validates both. |
| tests/RdpAudit.Core.Tests/LedgerChainVerifierTests.cs | The tamper-evidence contract, from both sides: a modified value, a row removed from the middle and two swapped rows each break the chain at a named entry, each is followed by a counterfactual that restores the original value and asserts the chain verifies again, pre-chain rows count as unverifiable rather than as tampering, and the digest is stable across DateTime kinds. |
| tests/RdpAudit.Core.Tests/StoreFingerprintChainTests.cs | The pure chain rules (B19, B23, B24): the digest covers every trusted field and is independent of table-count enumeration order, and Verify() distinguishes all FIVE states - a pristine chain is Ok (not Missing), an edited payload, a removed middle record and a rollback are ChainBroken, a deleted tail and a removed directory are Missing, an unreadable record or unreadable tip is Unreadable, and a tip that stops exactly one record short of a chain whose appended record a durable write intent names is Interrupted. The B24 cases pin the boundary rather than the happy path: without the intent the same shape is ChainBroken, an intent that names a different record, sequence, count or version is ChainBroken, a perfect intent cannot explain away an edited record, a deleted tail stays Missing, an unreadable file is not explained, and a stale intent over an agreeing tip is Ok and silent. The B23 cases pin the tip's field semantics: a replaced head sequence is ChainBroken with both numbers, a replaced sealing time changes no verdict and no message, and HeadDigest/EntryCount stay verified. The B22 cases pin that Canonicalize is total for nulls, that DescribeNullField names every field this layout always writes, and that Verify answers with a state for a hand-built null-bearing record. Baseline selection (newest clean shutdown first, newest periodic as the documented fallback) is pinned here too. |
| tests/RdpAudit.Core.Tests/StoreIntegrityComparerTests.cs | The offline-change detection rules with their false positives: a clean start reports nothing, retention deleting rows against a periodic baseline stays silent, an appended migration list stays silent, and rows disappearing, a rewritten migration history, an advanced ingestion sequence, a shorter ledger and a broken chain are all reported (with the benign explanation a restore produces). |
| tests/RdpAudit.Core.Tests/StorageAclAndEncryptionTests.cs | The storage ACL policy (the expected descriptor and its installer forms are accepted; a Users grant, enabled inheritance, a missing SYSTEM or Administrators ACE, missing inheritance flags and an unreadable descriptor are all rejected) and the encryption guard (None is silent, VolumeManaged warns that the volume cannot be verified, SqlCipher and an undefined value refuse to start). |
| tests/RdpAudit.Service.Tests/ApplicationDataPurgeSpaceReclamationTests.cs | The online space-reclamation policy: the statement list contains the WAL checkpoint and the bounded incremental vacuum and cannot contain a full VACUUM, the page budget is clamped, the report says honestly when nothing was released and why - and, on a real file, the purge does NOT shrink a store whose auto-vacuum mode is None while the same code does shrink one created in incremental mode. |
| tests/RdpAudit.Service.Tests/IpcDispatcherStorageHardeningTests.cs | The Stage 5 commands through the real dispatcher: a compaction request against the running service is refused with the offline procedure and audited, an empty purge payload is an invalid request rather than a full wipe, the dry run predicts exactly what the apply pass deletes, a wrong phrase and a legal hold both refuse and delete nothing, an IP/CIDR/login/window purge removes only the target rows and leaves the preserved surfaces intact, and a tampered ledger row is detected while the counterfactual passes. |
| tests/RdpAudit.Service.Tests/StoreFingerprintChainStoreTests.cs | The on-disk side of the chain (B19, B22, B23, B24): every case is a real file-system mutation an attacker with write access to the integrity folder could perform - delete the newest entry (Missing), delete a middle entry (ChainBroken), edit a payload (ChainBroken), corrupt the sealed envelope or replace it with plaintext (Unreadable) - plus the B24 write ordering (a capture that fails between appending its record and re-sealing the tip is Interrupted and mirrored to the sink; deleting the intent makes the identical shape ChainBroken; a forged, mismatching or unprotected intent is ignored; a perfect intent still cannot hide an edited record; the next successful capture repairs the store by itself and leaves no intent behind), the B23 tip semantics (a tip of another layout version is Unreadable; a replaced head sequence is ChainBroken), the B22 no-throw contract (an explicit null in every field this layout always writes is reported and never crashes; a mutation fuzz over every entry property is always a verdict and never Ok, with the one removal that a digest cannot see recorded as its own decision), a store written without any intent still verifying as before, the pre-B19 migration, the alarm mirror receiving the tip on a clean shutdown and nothing on a periodic capture, and the documented limit that removing BOTH stores leaves a shorter but self-consistent chain. |
| tests/RdpAudit.Service.Tests/StoreIntegrityServiceTests.cs | The durable fingerprint and the scheduled checks against a real SQLite file: a clean shutdown followed by an unchanged start reports nothing (twice), rows disappearing while stopped are reported and marked and audited, a clean capture clears a stale marker, the default pass journals the cheap checks only, the full check runs only inside the maintenance window, and a tampered ledger is journaled as a failure rather than a warning. |
| tests/RdpAudit.Configurator.Tests/StoreIntegrityRenderingTests.cs | B25 pure renderers, no window handle and no STA harness: the escalation rule for all five states plus the fail-closed cases (BandOf/BandForReport, wire names, phrases), the Database-tab report's "Integrity / tamper evidence" section with the verdict, state, entries, timestamps, marker, ledger chain and check outcomes, and the Diagnostic report's "Store integrity / tamper evidence" section - including an UNKNOWN verdict that names why (no completed StoreFingerprint section) and prints the section outcome, an Unavailable snapshot and a host that carries no integrity fields at all. |
| tests/RdpAudit.Service.Tests/StorageAclEnforcerTests.cs | The runtime ACL path on the real filesystem: applying the policy makes every covered folder compliant and creates them, the descriptor on disk carries no Users grant, a folder that grants Users is reported as drift by name, a missing tree is not compliant (nothing inspected proves nothing) and a blank root is refused. |
| tests/RdpAudit.Configurator.Tests/DatabaseCompactServiceTests.cs | The offline compaction end to end: a store that still occupies the space of deleted rows shrinks while every surviving row is preserved (quick_check, per-table counts, migration history and foreign keys verified), the pre-compaction file is kept with a receipt, and a missing path or an unreadable file is refused with the live store byte-identical. |
| tests/RdpAudit.Configurator.Tests/PurgeBySubjectDialogTests.cs | The purge dialog on the STA harness: Purge stays disabled until a dry run succeeded AND the exact phrase was typed, any deviation re-disables it, the preview never asks to apply and renders the counts plus the preserved list, applying sends the phrase and renders the applied report, and an unanswered call presents as nothing deleted rather than an empty success. |
